\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}}{n^{2}(n+m)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{1}(X)-f(X)\}^{2}]+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{2}^{-(1)}(X)-\widehat{f}_{2}(X)\}^{2}]

\displaystyle~~+\frac{m^{2}n}{n^{2}(n+m)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{2}^{-(1)}(X)-\widehat{f}_{2}(X)\}^{2}]+\frac{m}{(n+m)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{2}^{-(1)}(X)-\widehat{f}_{2}(X)\}^{2}]

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{2,1})^{2}]~\lesssim~

\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}}{n(n+m)^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{1}(X)-f(X)\}^{2}]+\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{2}n}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{2}^{-(1)}(X)-\widehat{f}_{2}(X)\}^{2}]

\displaystyle~+\frac{m^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}(n+m)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{2}^{-(1)}(X)-\widehat{f}_{2}(X)\}^{2}]+\frac{nm}{\sigma_{1}^{2}(n+m)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{2}^{-(1)}(X)-\widehat{f}_{2}(X)\}^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma_{1}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{1}(X)-f(X)\}^{2}]+\frac{m}{\sigma_{1}^{2}(n+m)}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{2}^{-(1)}(X)-\widehat{f}_{2}(X)\}^{2}].

The other terms
\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{2,i})^{2}]
for
i\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
can be similarly handled, which yields that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{2}]\}^{1/2}\{\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{2,i})^{2}]\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\bigl(M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+M_{2,f}^{1/2}\bigr)\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}^{1/2}+\sqrt{\frac{m}{n(n+m)}}\frac{\bigl(M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+M_{2,f}^{1/2}\bigr)}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{2}^{(-i)}(X)-\widehat{f}_{2}(X)\}^{2}]}

\displaystyle~+\sqrt{\frac{m}{n(n+m)}}\frac{\bigl(M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+M_{2,f}^{1/2}\bigr)}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\sum_{i=n_{0}+1}^{n}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{1}^{(-i)}(X)-\widehat{f}_{1}(X)\}^{2}]}

\displaystyle\lesssim~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\bigl(M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+M_{2,f}^{1/2}\bigr)\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle~+\frac{\bigl(M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+M_{2,f}^{1/2}\bigr)}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\bigg\{\frac{nm}{n+m}\bigg\}^{1/2}\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{(-i)}(X)-\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{cross}}(X)\}^{2}]\bigg\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim~

\displaystyle\frac{\bigl(M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+M_{2,f}^{1/2}\bigr)}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\bigl(\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}^{1/2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{Stability}}^{1/2}\bigr).

Next we deal with
\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{2,n+1})^{2}]
. Similarly as before, we have

\displaystyle\sigma\times(\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{2,n+1})

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{r}{n+m}\{\widehat{f}_{1}(X_{n+1})-f(X_{n+1})-\widehat{f}_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{n+1})+f(\widetilde{X}_{n+1})\}

\displaystyle~+\frac{rm}{n(n+m)}\sum_{i=n_{0}+1}^{n}\{\widehat{f}_{2}^{(-(n+1))}(X_{i})-\widehat{f}_{2}(X_{i})\}-\frac{r}{n+m}\sum_{i=n+m_{0}+1}^{n+m}\{\widehat{f}_{2}^{(-(n+1))}(X_{i})-\widehat{f}_{2}(X_{i})\}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{r}{n+m}\{\widehat{f}_{1}(X_{n+1})-f(X_{n+1})-\widehat{f}_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{n+1})+f(\widetilde{X}_{n+1})\},

where the last line uses our condition on
\widehat{f}_{2}
that it does not use the unlabeled dataset, thereby
\widehat{f}_{2}
and
\widehat{f}_{2}^{(-(n+1))}
remain the same. This leads to

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{2,n+1})^{2}]\leq\frac{2r^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{1}(X)-f(X)\}^{2}].

The other term
\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{2,i})^{2}]
for
i\in[n+m]\!\setminus\![n+1]
can be similarly handled, which yields that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=n+1}^{n+m}\{\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{2}]\}^{1/2}\{\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{2,i})^{2}]\}^{1/2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}M_{2,f}^{1/2}\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}^{1/2}.

Summing all the results from Analysis (I), (II) and (III), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{n}(U_{\mathrm{cross}}-\psi)}{\sqrt{\smash[b]{\Lambda_{n,m,f}}}}\leq t\biggr)-\Phi(t)\bigg|~\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{M_{3,\ell_{1}}+M_{3,f}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{1}^{3}}+\frac{(M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+M_{2,f}^{1/2}+\sigma_{1})\sigma_{\ell}}{\sqrt{n-r}\sigma_{1}^{2}}

(21)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+M_{2,f}^{1/2}+\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\bigl(\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}^{1/2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{Stability}}^{1/2}\bigr).

Obtaining the bound
\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}^{1/3}
.

To obtain the bound depending on
\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}^{1/3}
, the decomposition (
20
) together with
Lemma

4
yields

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{n}(U_{\mathrm{cross}}-\psi)}{\sqrt{\smash[b]{\Lambda_{n,m,f}}}}\leq t\biggr)-\Phi(t)\bigg|\ \lesssim\ \sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}\bigl(W+\Delta_{1}\leq t\bigr)-\Phi(t)\big|+\{\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{2}|^{2}]\}^{1/3}.

Moreover, following the previous analysis, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}\bigl(W+\Delta_{1}\leq t\bigr)-\Phi(t)\big|\ \lesssim\ \frac{M_{3,\ell_{1}}+M_{3,f}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{1}^{3}}+\frac{(M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+M_{2,f}^{1/2}+\sigma_{1})\sigma_{\ell}}{\sqrt{n-r}\sigma_{1}^{2}}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\{\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{2}|^{2}]\}^{1/3}\ \lesssim\ \frac{\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}^{1/3}}{\sigma_{1}^{2/3}}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{n}(U_{\mathrm{cross}}-\psi)}{\sqrt{\smash[b]{\Lambda_{n,m,f}}}}\leq t\biggr)-\Phi(t)\bigg|\ \lesssim\ \frac{M_{3,\ell_{1}}+M_{3,f}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{1}^{3}}+\frac{(M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+M_{2,f}^{1/2}+\sigma_{1})\sigma_{\ell}}{\sqrt{n-r}\sigma_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}^{1/3}}{\sigma_{1}^{2/3}}.

(22)

Conclusion.

Now combining the two inequalities (
21
) and (
22
) proves the desired claim in
Theorem

3
.

C.7
Proof of
Proposition

3

We prove the result focusing on the linear kernel
\ell(y)=y
. For notational simplicity, assume that we have the labeled dataset of size
2n
and the unlabeled dataset of size
2m
. Our target assistant-function
f
is set as
f(x)=0
for all
x
, and our estimators
\widehat{f}_{1}
and
\widehat{f}_{2}
are set as

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{1}(x)=\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon_{n}}}{2}\times\biggl(x+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\biggr)\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{f}_{2}(x)=\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon_{n}}}{2}\times\biggl(x+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=n+1}^{2n}X_{i}\biggr).

(23)

Assume that
Y
and
X
are perfectly correlated and follow the standard Normal distribution as
Y=X\sim N(0,1)
. Then it can be seen that

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}=\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{1}(X)-f(X)\}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{f}_{2}(X)-f(X)\}^{2}]=\epsilon_{n}.

Moreover, we can prove that
\Delta_{\mathrm{Stability}}=\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}=\epsilon_{n}
, from which we can verify the condition
\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}\geq\max\{\epsilon_{n},\Delta_{\mathrm{Stability}}\}
. Using these estimators and letting
\varepsilon_{n}:=\sqrt{\epsilon_{n}}/2
,

\displaystyle\overline{Y}:=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{2n}Y_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{Y}:=\frac{1}{2m}\sum_{i=2n+1}^{2n+2m}Y_{i},

the semi-supervised U-statistic
U_{\mathrm{cross}}
can be written as

\displaystyle U_{\mathrm{cross}}\,=\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(Y_{i}-\widehat{f}_{2}(X_{i}))+\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=n+1}^{2n}(Y_{i}-\widehat{f}_{1}(X_{i}))+\frac{1}{2n+2m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{f}_{2}(X_{i})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2n+2m}\sum_{i=2n+1}^{2n+m}\widehat{f}_{2}(X_{i})+\frac{1}{2n+2m}\sum_{i=n+1}^{2n}\widehat{f}_{1}(X_{i})+\frac{1}{2n+2m}\sum_{i=2n+m+1}^{2n+2m}\widehat{f}_{1}(X_{i})

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\biggl(1-\varepsilon_{n}\frac{m}{n+m}\biggr)\overline{Y}+\varepsilon_{n}\frac{m}{n+m}\widetilde{Y},

where we leverage the invariance of
U_{\mathrm{cross}}
to location-shifts for both
\widehat{f}_{1}
and
\widehat{f}_{2}
. That is,
U_{\mathrm{cross}}
remains the same for any values of
c_{1},c_{2}
in
\widehat{f}_{1}+c_{1}
and
\widehat{f}_{2}+c_{2}
. Hence, letting
Z_{1},Z_{2}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}N(0,1)
and noting that
\Lambda_{n,m,f}=\mathrm{Var}[Y]
in this example,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2n}U_{\mathrm{cross}}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}[Y]}}\overset{d}{=}\biggl(1-\varepsilon_{n}\frac{m}{n+m}\biggr)Z_{1}+\varepsilon_{n}\frac{m}{n+m}\sqrt{\frac{n}{m}}Z_{2}.

Letting
n/m:=\lambda\leq 1
, we characterize the distribution of the standardized
U_{\mathrm{cross}}
as

\displaystyle\biggl(1-\frac{m}{n+m}\varepsilon_{n}\biggr)Z_{1}+\varepsilon_{n}\frac{m}{n+m}\sqrt{\frac{n}{m}}Z_{2}\sim N\biggl(0,\biggl(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{1+\lambda}\biggr)^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{2}}\biggr).

Thus, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have the identity:

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{2n}U_{\mathrm{cross}}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}[Y]}}\leq t\biggr)-\Phi(t)\bigg|=\bigg|\Phi\biggl(t\bigg\{\biggl(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{1+\lambda}\biggr)^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{2}}\bigg\}^{-1/2}\biggr)-\Phi(t)\bigg|.

Take
t=1
. Then for sufficiently large
n
, we can guarantee that
\varepsilon_{n}\in(0,1/2)
is sufficiently small, ensuring that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{2n}U_{\mathrm{cross}}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}[Y]}}\leq t\biggr)-\Phi(t)\bigg|\,\geq\,

\displaystyle C_{1}\Bigg|1-\bigg\{\biggl(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{1+\lambda}\biggr)^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{2}}\bigg\}^{-1/2}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle C_{2}\bigg|\bigg\{\biggl(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{1+\lambda}\biggr)^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{2}}\bigg\}^{1/2}-1\bigg|

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle C_{2}\bigg|\sqrt{\frac{(1-\varepsilon_{n})^{2}+\lambda}{1+\lambda}}-1\bigg|=\frac{C_{2}}{\sqrt{1+\lambda}}(\sqrt{1+\lambda}-\sqrt{(1-\varepsilon_{n})^{2}+\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle\frac{C_{3}\varepsilon_{n}}{1+\lambda}\geq\frac{C_{3}}{2}\varepsilon_{n}.

The claim now follows by noting that
\varepsilon_{n}=\sqrt{\epsilon_{n}}/2\geq\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}^{1/2}/2
.

Remark 2
.

We specifically analyzed the estimators
\widehat{f}_{1}
and
\widehat{f}_{2}
presented in (
23
) to demonstrate a non-trivial role of
\Delta_{\mathrm{Stability}}
. In fact, the same proof goes through with the following simpler estimators

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{1}(x)=\widehat{f}_{2}(x)=\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon_{n}}{2}}x\quad\text{for all $x\in\mathbb{R}$,}

which satisfy
\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}=\epsilon_{n}
. Moreover, we have
\Delta_{\mathrm{Stability}}=0
as
\widehat{f}_{1}
and
\widehat{f}_{2}
are independent of the data. This implies that the same conclusion in
Theorem

3
also holds for the plug-in estimator
U_{\mathrm{plug}}
if we set
\widehat{f}(x)=\sqrt{\epsilon_{n}/2}x
in the definition of
U_{\mathrm{plug}}
.

C.8
Proof of
Theorem

4

We first remark that when
\widehat{f}
is conditioned,
U_{\mathrm{single}}
is essentially the oracle version of the semi-supervised U-statistic where
f
is unknown. Therefore the proof of
Theorem

3
remains valid for
U_{\mathrm{single}}
with the terms involving
\Delta_{\mathrm{MSPE}}
and
\Delta_{\mathrm{Stability}}
being zero. In particular, we have the following conditional guarantee:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{n}(U_{\mathrm{single}}-\psi)}{\sqrt{\smash[b]{\Lambda_{n,m,\widehat{f}}}}}\leq t\,\bigg|\,\widehat{f}\biggr)-\Phi(t)\bigg|\ \leq\

\displaystyle C\Bigg\{\frac{M_{3,\ell_{1}}+\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{f}(X)-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{f}(X)\,|\,\widehat{f}]|^{3}\,|\,\widehat{f}]}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{1}^{3}}

\displaystyle+\frac{(M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+\{\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{f}(X)-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{f}(X)\,|\,\widehat{f}]|^{2}\,|\,\widehat{f}]\}^{1/2}+\sigma_{1})\sigma_{\ell}}{\sqrt{n-r}\sigma_{1}^{2}}\Bigg\}.

Now by taking the expectation over
\widehat{f}
on both sides and using Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{n}(U_{\mathrm{single}}-\psi)}{\sqrt{\smash[b]{\Lambda_{n,m,\widehat{f}}}}}\leq t\biggr)-\Phi(t)\bigg|\ \leq\

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{n}(U_{\mathrm{single}}-\psi)}{\sqrt{\smash[b]{\Lambda_{n,m,\widehat{f}}}}}\leq t\,\bigg|\,\widehat{f}\biggr)-\Phi(t)\bigg|\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\

\displaystyle C\Bigg\{\frac{M_{3,\ell_{1}}+M_{3,\widehat{f}}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{1}^{3}}+\frac{(M_{2,\ell_{1}}^{1/2}+M_{2,\widehat{f}}^{1/2}+\sigma_{1})\sigma_{\ell}}{\sqrt{n-r}\sigma_{1}^{2}}\Bigg\}.

This completes the proof of
Theorem

4
.

C.9
Proof of
Theorem

5

In this proof, we begin by addressing a simple case and gradually increase the generality of the problem setting. In particular,
Section

C.9.1
focuses on the setting where the kernel
\ell
has order one and is uniformly bounded by some constant. We then extend this result to unbounded kernels of order one in
Section

C.9.2
. Lastly,
Section

C.9.3
extends the result to unbounded kernels of arbitrary order. By doing so, we can effectively convey the main idea behind the proof without complicating the notation from the beginning.

C.9.1
Simplest Case: Bounded Kernel of Order One

In this subsection, we assume that the kernel
\ell
has order one, i.e.,
\ell(y)=\ell_{1}(y)
. Given the distribution
P
of
(X,Y)
in the local asymptotic minimax lower bound, we also assume that the related quantities
|\ell_{1}(y)-\psi_{1}(x)|
and
|\psi_{1}(x)-\mathbb{E}_{P}[\psi_{1}(X)]|
where
\psi_{1}(\cdot)=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\ell_{1}(Y)\,|\,X=\cdot]
are uniformly bounded by some constant
K
for all values of
(x,y)
on the domain
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
of
(X,Y)
.

Given the density function
p
of
P
with respect to the Lebesgue measure
2
2

2

We assume this for notational convenience and the same proof holds for cases where the density is defined with respect to some other base measure.
, consider a tilted density
p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}
defined as

\displaystyle p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y):=p(x,y)\{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(x,y)\}\{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x)\}.

Here
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}
are some real numbers and
k_{1}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\mapsto\mathbb{R},\,k_{2}:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
are some functions. Writing the conditional density of
Y\,|\,X
and the marginal density of
X
as
p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}
and
p_{X}
, respectively, we assume that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Y}}k_{1}(x,y)p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y\,|\,x)dy=0\;\text{for all $x\in\mathcal{X}$ and }\int_{\mathcal{X}}k_{2}(x)p_{X}(x)dx=0.

(24)

Moreover, for some given
K>0
, assume that
\|k_{1}\|_{\infty}:=\sup_{(x,y)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}}|k_{1}(x,y)|\leq K
,
\|k_{2}\|_{\infty}:=\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}|k_{2}(x)|\leq K
,
|\epsilon_{1}|<1/K,|\epsilon_{2}|<1/K
so that
p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}
is a valid density function. The constructed tilted density
p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y)=p(x,y)k_{1}(x,y)\{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x)\},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y)=p(x,y)\{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(x,y)\}k_{2}(x),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y)=\frac{k_{1}(x,y)}{\{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(x,y)\}}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y)=\frac{k_{2}(x)}{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x)},

and these alternative expressions will be used through the proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y)dxdy=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}k_{1}(x,y)p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y\,|\,x)dy}_{=0}\{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x)\}p_{X}(x)dx=0,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y)dxdy=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(x,y)\}p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y\,|\,x)dy}_{=1}p_{X}(x)k_{2}(x)dx=0,

which implies that for any given
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X,Y)\biggr]=\mathbb{E}_{P_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X,Y)\biggr]=0,

where
\mathbb{E}_{P_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}
denotes the expectation with respect to
(X,Y)
from the distribution
P_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}
with density
p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}
. We also have that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X,Y)\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X,Y)\biggr]

(25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}k_{1}(x,y)p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y\,|\,x)dy}_{=0}k_{2}(x)p_{X}(x)dx=0.

Having presented some preliminary results, we now describe the specific setting that we consider:

•

Denote
Z_{n,m}:=\{(X_{i},Y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}\cup\{X_{i}\}_{i=n+1}^{n+m}
, which are mutually independent observations drawn from
(X,Y)\sim P_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}
and
X\sim P_{X,\epsilon_{2}}
with density
p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}
, which is the marginal density of
X
given as
p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(x)=p_{X}(x)\{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x)\}
.

•

Consider some generic estimator
\widehat{\psi}(Z_{n,m})=\widehat{\psi}
of the parameter

\displaystyle\psi(P_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}):=\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell(y)p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y)dydx.

The parameter is differentiable with respect to
\epsilon_{1}
and
\epsilon_{2}
, satisfying

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell(y)\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y)dydx,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell(y)\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y)dydx

under the additional assumption that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}|\ell(y)|p(x,y)dydx<\infty.

This additional condition allows us to interchange differentiation and integration. Note that this moment condition is fulfilled as we assume the
2+\upsilon
finite moment of
\ell
for
\upsilon>0
.

•

To apply the van Trees inequality, we specify the prior on
\epsilon_{1}
and
\epsilon_{2}
. More specifically, assume that
\epsilon_{1}
and
\epsilon_{2}
are independent and follow the same distribution with the cosine density

\displaystyle g(u)=\frac{1}{\delta}\cos^{2}\biggl(\frac{\pi u}{2\delta}\biggr)\quad\text{supported on $[-\delta,\delta]$,}

where
\delta\in(0,K^{-1})
will be specified later. Note that
g(\delta)=g(-\delta)=0
and

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\frac{\partial}{\partial u}g(u)du=0.

(26)

The specific choice of cosine density is not crucial. In fact, the proof follows for any centered prior distribution that satisfies conditions in the van Trees inequality
(e.g.,
Polyanskiy and Wu, 2023
, Theorem 29.3)
.

A lower bound for Bayes risk via van Trees inequality.

Now, the integration by parts under the condition that
g(\delta)=g(-\delta)=0
yields

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\Biggl[\prod_{i=1}^{n}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x_{i},y_{i})\prod_{j=n+1}^{n+m}p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(x_{j})g(\epsilon_{1})g(\epsilon_{2})\Biggr]d\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\biggl(\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr)\Biggl[\prod_{i=1}^{n}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x_{i},y_{i})\prod_{j=n+1}^{n+m}p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(x_{j})g(\epsilon_{1})g(\epsilon_{2})\Biggr]d\epsilon_{1}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\Biggl[\prod_{i=1}^{n}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x_{i},y_{i})\prod_{j=n+1}^{n+m}p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(x_{j})g(\epsilon_{1})g(\epsilon_{2})\Biggr]d\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\biggl(\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr)\Biggl[\prod_{i=1}^{n}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x_{i},y_{i})\prod_{j=n+1}^{n+m}p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(x_{j})g(\epsilon_{1})g(\epsilon_{2})\Biggr]d\epsilon_{2}.

Denoting the expectation taken over both
(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
and
Z_{n,m}
as
\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}
, these two identities show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}\biggl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})\underbrace{\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\biggl\{\log\biggl(\prod_{i=1}^{n}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X_{i},Y_{i})\prod_{j=n+1}^{n+m}p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(X_{j})g(\epsilon_{1})g(\epsilon_{2})\biggr)\biggr\}}_{:=\eta_{1}}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr]:=\tau_{1}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}\biggl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})\underbrace{\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\biggl\{\log\biggl(\prod_{i=1}^{n}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X_{i},Y_{i})\prod_{j=n+1}^{n+m}p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(X_{j})g(\epsilon_{1})g(\epsilon_{2})\biggr)\biggr\}}_{:=\eta_{2}}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr]:=\tau_{2}.

By letting
\bm{\eta}:=(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})^{\top},\bm{\tau}:=(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{\top}
and
\bm{u}:=(u_{1},u_{2})^{\top}
, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})^{2}\bigr]~\geq~

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{u}\neq 0}\frac{(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\tau})^{2}}{\bm{u}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\bm{\eta}\bm{\eta}^{\top}]\bm{u}}=\sup_{\bm{u}:\|\bm{u}\|_{2}=1}\bigl\{\bm{u}^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[\bm{\eta}\bm{\eta}^{\top}])^{-1/2}\bm{\tau}\bigr\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\bm{\tau}^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[\bm{\eta}\bm{\eta}^{\top}])^{-1}\bm{\tau}.

To explicitly compute the inverse of
\mathbb{E}[\bm{\eta}\bm{\eta}^{\top}]
, observe that

\displaystyle\eta_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X_{i},Y_{i})+\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log g(\epsilon_{1}).

Hence, using the condition (
26
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}[\eta_{1}^{2}]~=~

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},X,Y}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X,Y)\biggr)^{2}\biggr]+\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1}}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log g(\epsilon_{1})\biggr)^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},X,Y}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{k_{1}(X,Y)}{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(X,Y)}\biggr)^{2}\biggr]+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle:=\

\displaystyle nT_{\epsilon_{1}}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\delta^{2}}.

Next for
\eta_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\ =\

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X_{i},Y_{i})+\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(X_{j})+\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log g(\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(X_{i})+\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log g(\epsilon_{2}),

and therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}[\eta_{2}^{2}]\ =\

\displaystyle(n+m)\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{2},X}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{k_{2}(X)}{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(X)}\biggr)^{2}\biggr]+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle:=\

\displaystyle(n+m)T_{\epsilon_{2}}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\delta^{2}}.

For the off-diagonal term, we need to consider the expectation of
\eta_{1}\eta_{2}
, which turns out to be zero. Specifically, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}[\eta_{1}\eta_{2}]\ =\

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}\biggl[\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X_{i},Y_{i})+\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log g(\epsilon_{1})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\times\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(X_{j})+\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log g(\epsilon_{2})\bigg\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n+m}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X_{i},Y_{i})\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(X_{j})\biggr]

\displaystyle+\

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},X_{i},Y_{i}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X_{i},Y_{i})\biggr]\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log g(\epsilon_{2})\biggr]

\displaystyle+\

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n+m}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log g(\epsilon_{1})\biggr]\mathbb{E}_{X_{j},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(X_{j})\biggr]

\displaystyle+\

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log g(\epsilon_{1})\biggr]\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log g(\epsilon_{2})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle 0,

where we use the conditions that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log g(\epsilon_{1})\biggr]=\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log g(\epsilon_{2})\biggr]=0

and the below due to the identity (
25
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},X,Y}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X,Y)\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log p_{X,\epsilon_{2}}(X)\biggr]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},X,Y}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X,Y)\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\log p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(X,Y)\biggr]=0.

Putting things together yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})^{2}\bigr]~\geq

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{1}&\tau_{2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}nT_{\epsilon_{1}}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\delta^{2}}&0\\
0&(n+m)T_{\epsilon_{2}}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{1}\\
\tau_{2}\end{pmatrix}

(27)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\tau_{1}^{2}}{nT_{\epsilon_{1}}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\delta^{2}}}+\frac{\tau_{2}^{2}}{(n+m)T_{\epsilon_{2}}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\delta^{2}}},

where we recall that

\displaystyle\tau_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr],\quad\tau_{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr],

\displaystyle T_{\epsilon_{1}}=\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},X,Y}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{k_{1}(X,Y)}{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(X,Y)}\biggr)^{2}\biggr]\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle T_{\epsilon_{2}}=\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{2},X}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{k_{2}(X)}{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(X)}\biggr)^{2}\biggr].

A refined expression for the lower bound.

Now take
\delta=Kn^{-1/2}
and assume that
n>K^{4}
. Under this assumption, the choice of
\delta=Kn^{-1/2}
satisfies
\delta<K^{-1}
, which ensures that
p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}
is a valid density. Under this choice, we have
|1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(X,Y)|\geq 1-1/\sqrt{n}
and
|1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(X)|\geq 1-1/\sqrt{n}
with probability one, provided that
\|k_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq K
and
\|k_{2}\|_{\infty}\leq K
. Using this, we can verify that

\displaystyle T_{\epsilon_{1}}~=~

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\frac{k_{1}^{2}(x,y)}{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(x,y)}p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y\,|\,x)p_{X}(x)\{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x)\}g(\epsilon_{1})g(\epsilon_{2})d\epsilon_{1}d\epsilon_{2}dydx

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-1/\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}k_{1}^{2}(x,y)p(x,y)dydx

and

\displaystyle T_{\epsilon_{2}}~=~

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\frac{k_{2}^{2}(x)}{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x)}p_{X}(x)g(\epsilon_{2})d\epsilon_{2}dx~\leq~\frac{1}{1-1/\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}k_{2}^{2}(x)p_{X}(x)dx.

Observe that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}~=~

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell_{1}(y)\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y)dydx

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell_{1}(y)p(x,y)k_{1}(x,y)\{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x)\}dydx.

This observation together with
\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{2}}[\epsilon_{2}]=0
yields

\displaystyle\tau_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr]=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell_{1}(y)k_{1}(x,y)p(x,y)dydx.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\tau_{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr]=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell_{1}(y)k_{2}(x)p(x,y)dydx=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\psi_{1}(x)k_{2}(x)p_{X}(x)dx,

where
\psi_{1}(x)=\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell_{1}(y)p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y\,|\,x)dy
.

Relating Bayes risk to minimax risk.

Let
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})
be a random vector from the distribution
P
with density
p
without perturbation. Note that
\mathbb{E}[\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})k_{1}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})]=\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}k_{1}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})]
and
\mathbb{E}[\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})k_{2}(\widetilde{X})]=\mathbb{E}[\{\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})]\}k_{2}(\widetilde{X})]
due to our conditions for
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
in (
24
). Then the established expressions for
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},T_{\epsilon_{1}},T_{\epsilon_{2}}
and
\delta=Kn^{-1/2}
applied to the lower bound (
27
) yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq~

\displaystyle\frac{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}k_{1}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})]\bigr)^{2}}{\frac{n}{1-1/\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}[k_{1}^{2}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})]+nK^{-2}\pi^{2}}+\frac{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\{\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})]\}k_{2}(\widetilde{X})]\bigr)^{2}}{\frac{n+m}{1-1/\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}[k_{2}^{2}(\widetilde{X})]+nK^{-2}\pi^{2}},

which holds for any
n>K^{4}
,
k_{1}\in\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
k_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2}
where

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{1}=\bigg\{k:\int_{\mathcal{Y}}k(x,y)p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y\,|\,x)dy=0,\ \|k\|_{\infty}\leq K\bigg\}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{2}=\bigg\{k:\int_{\mathcal{X}}k(x)p_{X}(x)dx=0,\ \|k\|_{\infty}\leq K\bigg\}.

Thus for
n>K^{4}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{K}_{1},k_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})^{2}\bigr]\ \geq\

\displaystyle\sup_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{K}_{1}}\frac{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}k_{1}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})]\bigr)^{2}}{\frac{n}{1-1/\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}[k_{1}^{2}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})]+nK^{-2}\pi^{2}}

\displaystyle+\

\displaystyle\sup_{k_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\{\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})]\}k_{2}(\widetilde{X})]\bigr)^{2}}{\frac{n+m}{1-1/\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}[k_{2}^{2}(\widetilde{X})]+nK^{-2}\pi^{2}}.

Since we assume that
|\ell_{1}(y)-\psi_{1}(x)|
and
|\psi_{1}(x)-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})]|
are bounded by
K
for all
(x,y)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
so that
\ell_{1}(\cdot)-\psi_{1}(\cdot)\in\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
\psi_{1}(\cdot)-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})]\in\mathcal{K}_{2}
. Hence by taking
k_{1}(x,y)=\ell_{1}(y)-\psi_{1}(x)
and
k_{2}(x)=\psi_{1}(x)-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})]
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\psi}}\sup_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{K}_{1},k_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}n\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})^{2}\bigr]~\geq~\frac{\sigma_{1,P}^{4}}{\frac{1}{1-n^{-1/2}}\sigma_{1,P}^{2}+K^{-2}\pi^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{2,P}^{4}}{\frac{1+m/n}{1-n^{-1/2}}\sigma_{2,P}^{2}+K^{-2}\pi^{2}},

where we recall
\sigma_{1,P}^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\mathrm{Var}_{P}\{\ell_{1}(Y)\,|\,X\}]
and
\sigma_{2,P}^{2}=\mathrm{Var}_{P}[\psi_{1}(X)]
.

Connecting minimax risk with the class
\mathcal{F}_{P}(K/\sqrt{n})
.

Recall
m/n\rightarrow\lambda\in[0,\infty]
. Also note that for any
k_{1}\in\mathcal{K}_{1}
,
k_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2}
,
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\in[-K/\sqrt{n},K/\sqrt{n}]
, the corresponding tilted distribution
P_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}
belongs to
\mathcal{F}_{P}(K^{2}/\sqrt{n})
. Therefore, denoting that the parameter
\psi
based on a distribution
Q
as
\psi_{Q}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{P}(K^{2}/\sqrt{n})}n\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{Q})^{2}\bigr]\geq\frac{\sigma_{1,P}^{4}}{\frac{1}{1-n^{-1/2}}\sigma_{1,P}^{2}+2K^{-2}\pi^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{2,P}^{4}}{\frac{1+m/n}{1-n^{-1/2}}\sigma_{2,P}^{2}+2K^{-2}\pi^{2}},

as the supremum becomes larger when it is taken over a larger set. Consequently

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{P}(K^{2}/\sqrt{n})}n\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{Q})^{2}\bigr]\geq\frac{\sigma_{1,P}^{4}}{\sigma_{1,P}^{2}+2K^{-2}\pi^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{2,P}^{4}}{(1+\lambda)\sigma_{2,P}^{2}+2K^{-2}\pi^{2}},

which concludes

\displaystyle\liminf_{K\rightarrow\infty}\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{P}(K/\sqrt{n})}n\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{Q})^{2}\bigr]\geq\sigma_{1,P}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{2,P}^{2}}{1+\lambda}.

C.9.2
Extension: Unbounded Kernel of Order One

In the previous subsection, we assume that
\ell_{1}(\cdot)-\psi_{1}(\cdot)
and
\psi_{1}(\cdot)-\mathbb{E}_{P}[\psi_{1}(X)]
are uniformly bounded. We now relax this constraint. The proof remains the same up to here:

\displaystyle\sup_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{K}_{1},k_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},Z_{n,m}}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})^{2}\bigr]~\geq~

\displaystyle\sup_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{K}_{1}}\frac{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}k_{1}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})]\bigr)^{2}}{\frac{n}{1-1/\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}[k_{1}^{2}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})]+nK^{-2}\pi^{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\sup_{k_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\{\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})]\}k_{2}(\widetilde{X})]\bigr)^{2}}{\frac{n+m}{1-1/\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}[k_{2}^{2}(\widetilde{X})]+nK^{-2}\pi^{2}}.

Since
\ell_{1}(\cdot)-\psi_{1}(\cdot)
and
\psi_{1}(\cdot)-\mathbb{E}_{P}[\psi_{1}(X)]
are not necessarily bounded, we cannot set them to be
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
, respectively. Instead, define a truncated kernel
\widetilde{\ell_{1}}(y)=\ell_{1}(y)\mathds{1}(|\ell_{1}(y)|\leq K/2)
, and set

\displaystyle k_{1}(x,y)=\widetilde{\ell_{1}}(y)-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\ell_{1}}(Y)\,|\,X=x]:=\widetilde{\ell_{1}}(y)-\widetilde{\psi}_{1}(x),

which is uniformly bounded by
-K
and
K
. Under this choice of
k_{1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}k_{1}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})+\widetilde{\ell_{1}}(\widetilde{Y})-\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})+\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\widetilde{\psi}_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sigma_{1,P}^{2}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}\{\widetilde{\ell}_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})\}]}_{=V_{1,K}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}\{\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\widetilde{\psi}_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}]}_{=V_{2,K}}.

By sequentially applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, Hölder’s inequality, and Markov’s inequality, we have for any
\upsilon>0
,

\displaystyle V_{1,K}^{2}\leq\sigma_{1,P}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\{\widetilde{\ell_{1}}(\widetilde{Y})-\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})\}^{2}]~=~

\displaystyle\sigma_{1,P}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})\mathds{1}(|\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})|>K/2)\}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sigma_{1,P}^{2}\big\{\mathbb{E}[\ell_{1}^{2+2\upsilon}(\widetilde{Y})]\big\}^{\frac{1}{1+\upsilon}}\big\{\mathbb{P}\bigl(|\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})|>K/2\bigr)\big\}^{\frac{\upsilon}{1+\upsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sigma_{1,P}^{2}\big\{\mathbb{E}[\ell_{1}^{2+2\upsilon}(\widetilde{Y})]\big\}^{\frac{1}{1+\upsilon}}\biggl\{\frac{2\mathbb{E}[|\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})|]}{K}\biggr\}^{\frac{\upsilon}{1+\upsilon}}=O\bigl(K^{-\frac{\upsilon}{1+\upsilon}}\bigr),

where we assume that
\mathbb{E}[\ell_{1}^{2+2\upsilon}(\widetilde{Y})]<\infty
. By (conditional) Jensen’s inequality, we can similarly show that

\displaystyle V_{2,K}^{2}\leq\sigma_{1,P}^{2}\big\{\mathbb{E}[\ell_{1}^{2+2\upsilon}(\widetilde{Y})]\big\}^{\frac{1}{1+\upsilon}}\biggl\{\frac{2\mathbb{E}[|\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})|]}{K}\biggr\}^{\frac{\upsilon}{1+\upsilon}}=O\bigl(K^{-\frac{\upsilon}{1+\upsilon}}\bigr).

Now let us look at the term
\mathbb{E}[k_{1}^{2}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})]
. Using Hölder’s inequality as above,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[k_{1}^{2}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})]~=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})+\widetilde{\ell_{1}}(\widetilde{Y})-\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})+\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\widetilde{\psi}_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}^{2}]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sigma_{1,P}^{2}+\mathbb{E}[\{\widetilde{\ell_{1}}(\widetilde{Y})-\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})\}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\{\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\widetilde{\psi}_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}^{2}]

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}[\{\widetilde{\ell_{1}}(\widetilde{Y})-\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})\}\{\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\widetilde{\psi}_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}]+2\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}\{\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\widetilde{\psi}_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}]

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}[\{\widetilde{\ell_{1}}(\widetilde{Y})-\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})\}\{\ell_{1}(\widetilde{Y})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})\}]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sigma_{1,P}^{2}+O\bigl(K^{-\frac{\upsilon}{2(1+\upsilon)}}\bigr).

Similarly we let
\check{\psi}_{1}(x):=\psi_{1}(x)\mathds{1}(|\psi_{1}(x)|\leq K/2)
, and set

\displaystyle k_{2}(x)=\check{\psi}_{1}(x)-\mathbb{E}[\check{\psi}_{1}(\widetilde{X})],

which is uniformly bounded by
-K
and
K
. Under the assumption that
\mathbb{E}[\ell_{1}^{2+2\upsilon}(\widetilde{Y})]<\infty
, a similar calculation along with Jensen’s inequality shows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{1}(\widetilde{X})]\}k_{2}(\widetilde{X})]=\sigma_{2,P}^{2}+O\bigl(K^{-\frac{\upsilon}{1+\upsilon}}\bigr)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[k_{2}^{2}(\widetilde{X})]=\sigma_{2,P}^{2}+O\bigl(K^{-\frac{\upsilon}{2(1+\upsilon)}}\bigr).

Hence under the finite
2+\upsilon
moment condition for
\ell_{1}
with
\upsilon>0
, we have the same conclusion as

\displaystyle\liminf_{K\rightarrow\infty}\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{P}(K/\sqrt{n})}n\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{Q})^{2}\bigr]\geq\sigma_{1,P}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{2,P}^{2}}{1+\lambda}.

C.9.3
Extension: Unbounded Kernel of Arbitrary Order

Next we extend the previous result for unbounded kernels of order one to those of arbitrary order
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
.

Building insight focusing on
r=2
.

Starting with the case of
r=2
, suppose that

\displaystyle\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\dots\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell(y,y^{\prime})p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x,y)p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})dxdx^{\prime}dydy^{\prime},

where
\ell(y,y^{\prime})
is symmetric in its argument. In order to build upon the result in the previous section, especially (
27
), we only need to re-compute

\displaystyle\tau_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr]\quad\text{and}\quad\tau_{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr].

The other parts remain the same. Since
\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1}}[\epsilon_{1}]=\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{2}}[\epsilon_{2}]=0
and
\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1}}[\epsilon_{1}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{2}}[\epsilon_{2}^{2}]=\frac{(\pi^{2}-6)}{3\pi^{2}}\delta^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr]~=~

\displaystyle 2\int\dots\int\ell(y,y^{\prime})p(x,y)k_{1}(x,y)\{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x)\}p(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\times\{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\}\{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x^{\prime})\}g(\epsilon_{1})g(\epsilon_{2})dydy^{\prime}dxdx^{\prime}d\epsilon_{1}d\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2\int\dots\int\ell(y,y^{\prime})k_{1}(x,y)p(x,y)p(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\times\{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x^{\prime})+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x)+\epsilon_{2}^{2}k_{2}(x)k_{2}(x^{\prime})\}g(\epsilon_{2})dydy^{\prime}dxdx^{\prime}d\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2\int\dots\int\ell(y,y^{\prime})k_{1}(x,y)p(x,y)p(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})dydy^{\prime}dxdx^{\prime}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle 2\int\dots\int\ell(y,y^{\prime})k_{1}(x,y)p(x,y)p(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\epsilon_{2}^{2}k_{2}(x)k_{2}(x^{\prime})g(\epsilon_{2})dydy^{\prime}dxdx^{\prime}d\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2\int\int\ell_{1}(y)k_{1}(x,y)p(x,y)dxdy

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\frac{2(\pi^{2}-6)}{3\pi^{2}}\delta^{2}\int\dots\int\ell(y,y^{\prime})k_{1}(x,y)k_{2}(x)k_{2}(x^{\prime})p(x,y)p(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})dydy^{\prime}dxdx^{\prime},

where we recall
\ell_{1}(y)=\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y^{\prime})\,|\,Y=y]
. Hence under the condition for
k_{1}
in (
24
) and
\mathbb{E}[|\ell(Y,Y^{\prime})|]<\infty
, we observe

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr]=2\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(Y)-\psi_{1}(X)\}k_{1}(X,Y)]+O(\delta^{2}K^{3}).

Next we similarly observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr]~=~

\displaystyle 2\int\dots\int\ell(y,y^{\prime})p(x,y)\{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(x,y)\}k_{2}(x)p(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\times\{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\}\{1+\epsilon_{2}k_{2}(x^{\prime})\}g(\epsilon_{1})g(\epsilon_{2})dydy^{\prime}dxdx^{\prime}d\epsilon_{1}d\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2\int\dots\int\ell(y,y^{\prime})p(x,y)\{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(x,y)\}k_{2}(x)p(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\times\{1+\epsilon_{1}k_{1}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\}g(\epsilon_{1})dydy^{\prime}dxdx^{\prime}d\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2\int\int\ell_{1}(y)k_{2}(x)p(x,y)dxdy

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle 2\int\dots\int\ell(y,y^{\prime})p(x,y)k_{2}(x)p(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\epsilon_{1}^{2}k_{1}(x,y)k_{1}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})g(\epsilon_{1})dydy^{\prime}dxdx^{\prime}d\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2\int\int\ell_{1}(y)k_{2}(x)p(x,y)dxdy

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\frac{2(\pi^{2}-6)}{3\pi^{2}}\delta^{2}\int\dots\int\ell(y,y^{\prime})p(x,y)k_{2}(x)p(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})k_{1}(x,y)k_{1}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})dydy^{\prime}dxdx^{\prime}.

Therefore, using the condition for
k_{2}
in (
24
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr]~=~2\mathbb{E}[\{\psi_{1}(Y)-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{1}(Y)]\}k_{2}(X)]+O(\delta^{2}K^{3}).

By taking
\delta=K/\sqrt{n}
, we will get the same asymptotic lower bound with an additional constant factor
4
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\liminf_{K\rightarrow\infty}\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{P}(K/\sqrt{n})}n\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{Q})^{2}\bigr]\geq 4\sigma_{1,P}^{2}+\frac{4\sigma_{2,P}^{2}}{1+\lambda}.

Arbitrary
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
.

Now suppose that
\ell(y_{1},\ldots,y_{r})
is symmetric in its arguments with a fixed
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and we are interested in estimating

\displaystyle\psi=\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{r})].

By symmetry of
\ell
in its arguments, we can write

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}=r\int\dots\int\ell(y_{1},\ldots,y_{r})\bigg\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x_{1},y_{1})\bigg\}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x_{2},y_{2})\cdots p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x_{r},y_{r})dx_{1}dy_{1}\cdots dx_{r}dy_{r},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}=r\int\dots\int\ell(y_{1},\ldots,y_{r})\bigg\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x_{1},y_{1})\bigg\}p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x_{2},y_{2})\cdots p_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x_{r},y_{r})dx_{1}dy_{1}\cdots dx_{r}dy_{r}.

Further write
\ell_{1}(y)=\mathbb{E}[\ell(y,Y_{2},\ldots,Y_{r})]
and
\psi_{1}(x)=\mathbb{E}[\ell_{1}(Y)\,|\,X=x]
. We may follow the analysis for the case of
r=2
and it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr]~=~r\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(Y)-\psi_{1}(X)\}k_{1}(X,Y)]+O(\delta^{2}K^{2r-1})\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}\psi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\biggr]~=~r\mathbb{E}[\{\psi_{1}(X)-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{1}(X)]\}k_{2}(X)]+O(\delta^{2}K^{2r-1}),

assuming that
\delta
is sufficiently small and
r
is fixed. Now, by taking
\delta=K/\sqrt{n}
, we will get the same asymptotic lower bound with an additional constant factor
r^{2}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\liminf_{K\rightarrow\infty}\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{P}(K/\sqrt{n})}n\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl[(\widehat{\psi}-\psi_{Q})^{2}\bigr]\geq r^{2}\sigma_{1,P}^{2}+\frac{r^{2}\sigma_{2,P}^{2}}{1+\lambda}.

C.10
Proof of
Proposition

4

Throughout the proof, we often omit the dependence on
P
in
\mathbb{E}_{P}
,
\mathrm{Var}_{P}
,
\mathrm{Cov}_{P}
,
G_{P,m,n}
and
H_{P,m,n}
to simplify the notation. To start, we observe that the variance of
U^{\star}_{\mathrm{adapt}}
without the scaling factor
(n+m)/(n+m-1)
is equal to that of

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n+m}\Biggl[\,\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl\{\frac{\delta_{i}}{n}\phi_{k}(Y_{i})-\frac{\delta_{i}}{n}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]+\frac{1}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\biggr\}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\times\biggl\{\frac{\delta_{j}}{n}\phi_{k}(Y_{j})-\frac{\delta_{j}}{n}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}]+\frac{1}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}]\biggr\}\Biggr].

Denoting the summands as

\displaystyle a_{ij}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl\{\frac{\delta_{i}}{n}\phi_{k}(Y_{i})-\frac{\delta_{i}}{n}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]+\frac{1}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\biggr\}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\times\biggl\{\frac{\delta_{j}}{n}\phi_{k}(Y_{j})-\frac{\delta_{j}}{n}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}]+\frac{1}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}]\biggr\},

for
1\leq i\neq j\leq n+m
, notice that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\Biggl[\sum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n+m}a_{ij}\Biggr]~=~

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n+m}\sum_{1\leq s\neq t\leq n+m}\mathrm{Cov}(a_{ij},a_{st})

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 4\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i,j,s\leq n+m\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Cov}(a_{ij},a_{is})+2\sum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n+m}\mathrm{Cov}(a_{ij},a_{ij})

\displaystyle:=~

\displaystyle 4S_{1}+2S_{2},

where the second identity holds since
\mathrm{Cov}(a_{ij},a_{st})=0
when
\{i,j\}\cap\{s,t\}=\emptyset
. We now analyze the two summations separately.

Analysis of
S_{1}
.
By the law of total covariance,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}(a_{ij},a_{is})~=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\underbrace{\mathrm{Cov}(a_{ij},a_{is}\,|\,X_{i},Y_{i})}_{=0}\bigr]+\mathrm{Cov}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[a_{ij}\,|\,X_{i},Y_{i}],\mathbb{E}[a_{is}\,|\,X_{i},Y_{i}]\bigr)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[a_{ij}\,|\,X_{i},Y_{i}],\mathbb{E}[a_{is}\,|\,X_{i},Y_{i}]\bigr)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{1}{n^{2}(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,Y_{1}\bigr\}-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,X_{1}\bigr\}\bigr]&\quad\text{if $\delta_{i}=1$,}\\[5.0pt]
\frac{1}{(n+m)^{4}}\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell_{2}(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,X_{1}\bigr\}\bigr]&\quad\text{if $\delta_{i}=0$.}\end{cases}

Therefore,
S_{1}
can be computed as

\displaystyle S_{1}~=~

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j,s\leq n+m\\
i,j,s\,\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Cov}(a_{ij},a_{is})+\sum_{i=n+1}^{n+m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j,s\leq n+m\\
i,j,s\,\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Cov}(a_{ij},a_{is})

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{n(n+m-1)(n+m-2)}{n^{2}(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}\biggl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,Y_{1}\bigr\}-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,X_{1}\bigr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\frac{m(n+m-1)(n+m-2)}{(n+m)^{4}}\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,X_{1}\bigr\}\bigr].

This can be further simplified as

\displaystyle S_{1}~=~

\displaystyle\frac{(n+m-1)(n+m-2)}{n(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,Y_{1}\bigr\}\bigr]

\displaystyle-~

\displaystyle\frac{m(n+m-1)(n+m-2)}{n(n+m)^{3}}\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,X_{1}\bigr\}\bigr]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{(n+m-1)(n+m-2)}{n(n+m)^{2}}\biggl\{\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,Y_{1}\bigr\}\bigr]-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,X_{1}\bigr\}\bigr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\biggl\{\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,Y_{1}\bigr\}\bigr]-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,X_{1}\bigr\}\bigr]\biggr\}\{1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\}.

Here and hereafter, we use the notation
o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
to represent a sequence of numbers that converges to zero as
n\rightarrow\infty
uniformly over
\mathcal{P}
.

Analysis of
S_{2}
.
Next for
S_{2}
, note that

\displaystyle S_{2}~=~

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq j\neq i\leq n+m}\mathrm{Var}(a_{ij})+\sum_{i=n+1}^{n+m}\sum_{1\leq j\neq i\leq n+m}\mathrm{Var}(a_{ij})

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n(n-1)\mathrm{Var}(a_{ij}\,|\,\delta_{i}=1,\delta_{j}=1)+nm\mathrm{Var}(a_{ij}\,|\,\delta_{i}=1,\delta_{j}=0)

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle mn\mathrm{Var}(a_{ij}\,|\,\delta_{i}=0,\delta_{j}=1)+m(m-1)\mathrm{Var}(a_{ij}\,|\,\delta_{i}=0,\delta_{j}=0),

where
\mathrm{Var}(a_{ij}\,|\,\delta_{i}=1,\delta_{j}=1)
denotes the variance of
a_{ij}
when
\delta_{i}=1,\delta_{j}=1
and the other terms are similarly defined. These variances are computed as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(a_{ij}

\displaystyle\,|\,\delta_{i}=1,\delta_{j}=1)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{4}}\mathrm{Var}\bigg\{\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl(\phi_{k}(Y_{1})-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{1})\,|\,X_{1}]\biggr)\biggl(\phi_{k}(Y_{2})-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{2})\,|\,X_{2}]\biggr)\bigg\},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(a_{ij}

\displaystyle\,|\,\delta_{i}=0,\delta_{j}=1)=\mathrm{Var}(a_{ij}\,|\,\delta_{i}=1,\delta_{j}=0)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}\bigg\{\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl(\phi_{k}(Y_{1})-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{1})\,|\,X_{1}]\biggr)\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{2})\,|\,X_{2}]\bigg\}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(a_{ij}

\displaystyle\,|\,\delta_{i}=0,\delta_{j}=0)=\frac{1}{(n+m)^{4}}\mathrm{Var}\bigg\{\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{1})\,|\,X_{1}]\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{2})\,|\,X_{2}]\bigg\}.

Therefore
S_{2}
can be written as

\displaystyle S_{2}~=~

\displaystyle\frac{n(n-1)}{n^{4}}\mathrm{Var}\bigg\{\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl(\phi_{k}(Y_{1})-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{1})\,|\,X_{1}]\biggr)\biggl(\phi_{k}(Y_{2})-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{2})\,|\,X_{2}]\biggr)\bigg\}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\frac{2mn}{n^{2}(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}\biggl\{\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl(\phi_{k}(Y_{1})-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{1})\,|\,X_{1}]\biggr)\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{2})\,|\,X_{2}]\biggr\}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\frac{m(m-1)}{(n+m)^{4}}\mathrm{Var}\biggl\{\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{1})\,|\,X_{1}]\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{2})\,|\,X_{2}]\biggr\}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle S_{2}~=~

\displaystyle\frac{m(m-1)}{(n+m)^{4}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]+\frac{2mn}{n^{2}(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}\bigg\{\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})-\frac{m}{n+m}\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})\bigg\}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\frac{n(n-1)}{n^{4}}\mathrm{Var}\bigg\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})-\frac{m}{n+m}\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})-\frac{m}{n+m}\ell_{1}(X_{1},Y_{2})+\frac{m^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})\bigg\}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigg\{\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})-\frac{m}{n+m}\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})\bigg\}~=~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]+\frac{m^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]

\displaystyle-~

\displaystyle\frac{2m}{n+m}\underbrace{\mathrm{Cov}\big\{\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2}),\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})\big\}}_{=\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]-\frac{m(m+2n)}{(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]

and an analogous calculation shows that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigg\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})-\frac{m}{n+m}\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})-\frac{m}{n+m}\ell_{1}(X_{1},Y_{2})+\frac{m^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})\bigg\}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]+\frac{2m^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]+\frac{m^{4}}{(n+m)^{4}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]

\displaystyle~-\frac{4m}{n+m}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]+\frac{4m^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]-\frac{4m^{3}}{(n+m)^{3}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]+\biggl[\frac{2m^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}-\frac{4m}{n+m}\biggr]\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]

\displaystyle~+\biggl[\frac{m^{4}}{(n+m)^{4}}+\frac{4m^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}-\frac{4m^{3}}{(n+m)^{3}}\biggr]\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]-\frac{2m(m+2n)}{(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]+\frac{m^{2}(m+2n)^{2}}{(m+n)^{4}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})].

Hence,
S_{2}
can be written as

\displaystyle S_{2}~=~

\displaystyle\frac{n-1}{n^{3}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]-\frac{2m\{m(n-1)+n(n-2)\}}{n^{3}(m+n)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\frac{m\{m^{2}(n-1)+mn(n-3)-n^{2}\}}{n^{3}(m+n)^{3}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]\{1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\}-\frac{2m}{n^{2}(n+m)}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]\{1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\frac{m^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]\{1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\}-\frac{m}{n(n+m)^{3}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\bigg[\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]-\frac{2m}{(n+m)}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]+\frac{m^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]\bigg]\{1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\}

\displaystyle-~

\displaystyle\frac{m}{n(n+m)^{3}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})].

Observe that
\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]\leq\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]\leq\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]
, which can be verified by Jensen’s inequality. Given this, when
\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]\leq C_{1}
and
\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]-2\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]+\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]\geq C_{2}
, we observe that

\displaystyle G_{m,n}~=~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]-\frac{2m}{(n+m)}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]+\frac{m^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigg\{\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl(\phi_{k}(Y_{1})-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{1})\,|\,X_{1}]\biggr)\biggl(\phi_{k}(Y_{2})-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{2})\,|\,X_{2}]\biggr)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]-2\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]+\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]\,\geq\,C_{2}.

Therefore for any
P\in\mathcal{P}
,

\displaystyle S_{2}~=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}G_{m,n}\{1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\}-\frac{m}{n(n+m)^{3}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}G_{m,n}\{1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\}.

In other words,
S_{2}
approximates
n^{-2}G_{m,n}\{1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\}
, regardless of the value of
m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}
.

Summary.
Recalling

\displaystyle H_{m,n}=\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,Y_{1}\bigr\}\bigr]-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,X_{1}\bigr\}\bigr],

we have shown that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}[U_{\mathrm{adapt}}^{\star}]~=~

\displaystyle\biggl(\frac{n+m-1}{n+m}\biggr)^{2}\{4S_{1}+2S_{2}\}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\biggl(\frac{4}{n}H_{m,n}+\frac{2}{n^{2}}G_{m,n}\biggr)\{1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\}.

with no restriction on
m
. Therefore, it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\biggl|\frac{\mathrm{Var}_{P}[U_{\mathrm{adapt}}^{\star}]}{4n^{-1}H_{P,m,n}+2n^{-2}G_{P,m,n}}-1\biggr|=0.

C.11
Proof of
Theorem

6

As in
Section

C.10
, we often omit the dependence on
P
whenever it is clear from the context. We again use the notation
a_{n}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(b_{n})
to denote that
a_{n}/b_{n}
converges to zero as
n\rightarrow\infty
uniformly over
\mathcal{P}
.

For simplicity, write

\displaystyle\Gamma_{k}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\phi_{k}(Y_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\big\}+\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{j=1}^{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}],

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}_{k}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\phi_{k}(Y_{i})-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\big\}+\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{j=1}^{n+m}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}],

\displaystyle\Pi_{i,k}:=\frac{\delta_{i}}{n}\phi_{k}(Y_{i})-\frac{\delta_{i}}{n}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]+\frac{1}{n+m}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\widehat{\Pi}_{i,k}:=\frac{\delta_{i}}{n}\phi_{k}(Y_{i})-\frac{\delta_{i}}{n}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]+\frac{1}{n+m}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}].

Then the difference between
U_{\mathrm{adapt}}
and
U_{\mathrm{adapt}}^{\star}
can be written as

\displaystyle U_{\mathrm{adapt}}-U_{\mathrm{adapt}}^{\star}~=~

\displaystyle\frac{n+m}{n+m-1}\Biggl[\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\bigl(\Gamma_{k}-\widehat{\Gamma}_{k}\bigr)^{2}+2\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\Gamma_{k}\bigl(\widehat{\Gamma}_{k}-\Gamma_{k}\bigr)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{i,k}-\Pi_{i,k}\bigr)^{2}\biggr\}-2\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{i,k}-\Pi_{i,k}\bigr)\Pi_{i,k}\biggr\}\Biggr]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{n+m}{n+m-1}\bigl[(\mathrm{I})+2(\mathrm{II})-(\mathrm{III})-2(\mathrm{IV})\bigr].

We shall show that each of
\mathbb{E}[|(\mathrm{I})|]
,
\mathbb{E}[|(\mathrm{II})|]
,
\mathbb{E}[|(\mathrm{III})|]
and
\mathbb{E}[|(\mathrm{IV})|]
is
o_{\mathcal{P}}(\{\mathrm{Var}[U_{\mathrm{adapt}}^{\star}]\}^{1/2})
. Then the desired claim follows since
(n+m)/(n+m-1)=1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
.

For the first term (I), we follow a similar approach in the proof of
Theorem

1
and show

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|(\mathrm{I})|]~=~

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\Gamma_{k}-\widehat{\Gamma}_{k}\bigr)^{2}\bigr]=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\Biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\bigr\}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\bigl\{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\big\}\Biggr)^{2}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C\times\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\big\{\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y)\,|\,X]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y)\,|\,X]\big\}^{2}\bigr],

where
C
denotes some positive constant. The last quantity multiplied by
n
can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y)\,|\,X]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y)\,|\,X]\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\ell(y_{1},y_{2})\bigl\{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)-\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)\bigr\}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\times\bigl\{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)-\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)\bigr\}p_{X}(x)d\nu(x)d\nu(y_{1})d\nu(y_{2})\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\biggl\{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell^{2}(y_{1},y_{2})p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)d\nu(y_{1})\biggr\}^{1/2}\biggl\{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\frac{\bigl\{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)-\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)\bigr\}^{2}}{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)}d\nu(y_{1})\biggr\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle~~~~~~\times\big|p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)-\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)\big|p_{X}(x)d\nu(y_{1})d\nu(y_{2})d\nu(x)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\int_{\mathcal{X}}\biggl\{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell^{2}(y_{1},y_{2})p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)d\nu(y_{1})d\nu(y_{2})\biggr\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle~~~\times\biggl\{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\frac{\bigl\{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)-\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)\bigr\}^{2}}{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)}d\nu(y_{1})\biggr\}^{1/2}\biggl\{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\frac{\bigl\{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)-\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)\bigr\}^{2}}{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)}d\nu(y_{2})\biggr\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle~~~\times p_{X}(x)d\nu(x)\Biggr]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}\biggl\{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell^{2}(y_{1},y_{2})p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)d\nu(y_{1})d\nu(y_{2})\biggr\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~\times\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\frac{\bigl\{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)-\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)\bigr\}^{2}}{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)}d\nu(y_{1})\biggr]\times p_{X}(x)d\nu(x)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\frac{\bigl\{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)-\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)\bigr\}^{2}}{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)}d\nu(y_{1})}_{=D_{\chi^{2}}(p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x},\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x})}\biggr]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~\times\Biggl\{\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell^{2}(y_{1},y_{2})p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)p_{X}(x)d\nu(y_{1})d\nu(y_{2})d\nu(x)\Biggr\}^{1/2}

where each step follows by applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality as well as the Fubini–Tonelli theorem. Observing that for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle-\ell(y_{1},y_{1})-\ell(y_{2},y_{2})\leq 2\ell(y_{1},y_{2})\leq\ell(y_{1},y_{1})+\ell(y_{2},y_{2}),

which yields that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell^{2}(y_{1},y_{2})p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)p_{X}(x)d\nu(y_{1})d\nu(y_{2})d\nu(x)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell^{2}(y_{1},y_{1})p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{1}\,|\,x)\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y_{2}\,|\,x)d\nu(y_{2})}_{=1}p_{X}(x)d\nu(y_{1})d\nu(x)=\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)].

Consequently, we have established that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y)\,|\,X]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y)\,|\,X]\bigr)^{2}\bigr]~\leq~

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[D_{\chi^{2}}(p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x},\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x})\bigr]\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle o_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]}\bigr),

under the condition that
\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[D_{\chi^{2}}(p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x},\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x})\bigr]=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
. This implies that
\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{I})]=o_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(n^{-1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]}\bigr)
.

For the second term
(\mathrm{II})
, we express it as the sum of
(\mathrm{II})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{II})_{2}
:

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})~=~

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\Gamma_{k}\bigl(\widehat{\Gamma}_{k}-\Gamma_{k}\bigr)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\phi_{k}(Y_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\big\}+\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{j=1}^{n+m}\big\{\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}]-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y)]\big\}\biggr)

\displaystyle\underbrace{\times\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]-\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{j=1}^{n+m}\bigl\{\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}]\bigr\}\biggr)}_{=(\mathrm{II})_{1}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y)]\times\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]

\displaystyle\underbrace{~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{j=1}^{n+m}\bigl\{\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}]\bigr\}\biggr)}_{=(\mathrm{II})_{2}}.

Observe that the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality yields

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})_{1}^{2}~\leq~

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})\times\Biggl[\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\phi_{k}(Y_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\big\}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{j=1}^{n+m}\big\{\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}]-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y)]\big\}\biggr)^{2}\Biggr]

and there exists some constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\phi_{k}(Y_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\big\}+\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{j=1}^{n+m}\big\{\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y_{j})\,|\,X_{j}]-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y)]\big\}\biggr)^{2}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n}\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)].

Therefore, combining with the previous result
\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{I})]=o_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(n^{-1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]}\bigr)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|(\mathrm{II})_{1}|]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{I})]}\times\sqrt{Cn^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]}=o_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(n^{-1}\{\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]\}^{3/4}\bigr).

Next we again follow an analogous approach in the proof of
Theorem

1
and show

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\mathrm{II})_{2}^{2}\bigr]\leq\frac{C}{n}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bigg\{\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y)]\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y)\,|\,X]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y)\,|\,X]\bigr)\bigg\}^{2}\biggr].

Recall that
\psi_{1}(x)=\mathbb{E}[\ell_{1}(Y)\,|\,X=x]
. The Cauchy–Schwarz inequality then yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bigg\{\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y)]\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(Y)\,|\,X]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\phi_{k}(Y)\,|\,X]\bigr)\bigg\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathcal{X}}\biggl\{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\bigl\{\ell_{1}(y)-\psi_{1}(x)\bigr\}\bigl\{p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y\,|\,x)-\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y\,|\,x)\bigr\}d\nu(y)\biggr\}^{2}p_{X}(x)d\nu(x)\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathcal{X}}\biggl\{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\bigl\{\ell_{1}(y)-\psi_{1}(x)\bigr\}^{2}p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y\,|\,x)d\nu(y)\biggr\}\times D_{\chi^{2}}\bigl(p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x},\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x}\bigr)p_{X}(x)d\nu(x)\biggr]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}\biggl\{\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\bigl\{\ell_{1}(y)-\psi_{1}(x)\bigr\}^{2}p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX}(y\,|\,x)d\nu(y)\biggr\}\times\mathbb{E}\Bigl[D_{\chi^{2}}\bigl(p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x},\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x}\bigr)\Bigr]p_{X}(x)d\nu(x)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[D_{\chi^{2}}(p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x},\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x})\bigr]\times\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(Y)-\psi_{1}(X)\}^{2}].

Hence, under the condition that
\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[D_{\chi^{2}}(p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x},\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x})\bigr]=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|(\mathrm{II})_{2}|]=o_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(\sqrt{n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(Y)-\psi_{1}(X)\}^{2}]}\bigr),

which in turn implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|(\mathrm{II})|]=o_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(n^{-1}\{\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]\}^{3/4}+\sqrt{n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(Y)-\psi_{1}(X)\}^{2}]}\bigr).

For the term
(\mathrm{III})
, we observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|(\mathrm{III})|]~=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{i,k}-\Pi_{i,k}\bigr)^{2}\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\mathrm{I})\bigr]\leq C^{\prime\prime}n^{-1}\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[D_{\chi^{2}}(p_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x},\widehat{p}_{Y\mkern 1.0mu|\mkern 1.0muX=x})\bigr]\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]},

where
C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}
are some positive constants. Therefore, we have
\mathbb{E}[|(\mathrm{III})|]=o_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(n^{-1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]}\bigr).

For the last term
(\mathrm{IV})
, applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality twice yields

\displaystyle(\mathrm{IV})^{2}~=~

\displaystyle\Biggl[\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{i,k}-\Pi_{i,k}\bigr)\Pi_{i,k}\biggr\}\Biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\Biggl[\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{i,k}-\Pi_{i,k}\bigr)^{2}\Biggr\}^{1/2}\Biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\Pi_{i,k}^{2}\biggr\}^{1/2}\Biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\Biggl[\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{i,k}-\Pi_{i,k}\bigr)^{2}\biggr\}\Biggr]}_{\mathrm{(III)}}\times\Biggl[\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\Pi_{i,k}^{2}\biggr\}\Biggr]

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\Pi_{i,k}^{2}\biggr\}\Biggr]\leq C\times\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)].

This implies by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|(\mathrm{IV})|]=o_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(n^{-1}\{\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]\}^{3/4}\bigr).

Combining all the ingredients yields that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\bigl[\big|U_{\mathrm{adapt}}-U_{\mathrm{adapt}^{\star}}\big|\bigr]}{\sqrt{n^{-1}H_{m,n}+n^{-2}G_{m,n}}}~=~

\displaystyle o_{\mathcal{P}}\Biggl(\sqrt{\frac{n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(Y)-\psi_{1}(X)\}^{2}]+n^{-2}\{\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]\}^{3/2}}{n^{-1}H_{m,n}+n^{-2}G_{m,n}}}\Biggr)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

where the second identity holds since

\displaystyle H_{m,n}~=~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,Y_{1}\bigr\}\bigr]-\frac{m}{n+m}\mathrm{Var}\bigl[\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})\,|\,X_{1}\bigr\}\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}\{\ell_{1}(Y)\,|\,X\}]=\mathbb{E}[\{\ell_{1}(Y)-\psi_{1}(X)\}^{2}]

and

\displaystyle G_{m,n}~=~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]-\frac{2m}{(n+m)}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]+\frac{m^{2}}{(n+m)^{2}}\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]

\displaystyle\geq~

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}[\ell(Y_{1},Y_{2})]-2\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{1}(Y_{1},X_{2})]+\mathrm{Var}[\ell_{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]\geq C\{\mathbb{E}[\ell(Y,Y)]\}^{3/2},

for some positive constant
C>0
under the moment conditions in the theorem. Hence, the desired result follows by
Proposition

4
.

C.12
Proof of
Corollary

2

The proof follows similar lines of that of
Theorem

6
. As in the proof of
Theorem

6
in
Section

C.11
, we often omit the dependence on
P
. We also express the difference between
U_{\mathrm{adapt}}-U_{\mathrm{adapt}}^{\star}
as

\displaystyle U_{\mathrm{adapt}}-U_{\mathrm{adapt}}^{\star}=\frac{n+m}{n+m-1}\bigl[(\mathrm{I})+2(\mathrm{II})-(\mathrm{III})-2(\mathrm{IV})\bigr],

where each term can be recalled in
Section

C.11
. According to
Proposition

4
, when
\ell(y_{1},y_{2})=y_{1}y_{2}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\biggl|\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P}[(U_{\mathrm{adapt}}^{\star}-\mu_{P}^{2})^{2}]}{4n^{-1}\mu_{P}^{2}\sigma_{m,n}^{2}+2n^{-2}\sigma_{m,n}^{2}}-1\biggr|=0.

Hence, to prove the claim of
Corollary

2
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P}[(U_{\mathrm{adapt}}-U_{\mathrm{adapt}}^{\star})^{2}]}{4n^{-1}\mu_{P}^{2}\sigma_{m,n}^{2}+2n^{-2}\sigma_{m,n}^{2}}=0,

(28)

or equivalently each of
\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{I})^{2}]
,
\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{II})^{2}]
,
\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{III})^{2}]
and
\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{IV})^{2}]
is
o_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(4n^{-1}\mu_{P}^{2}\sigma_{m,n}^{2}+2n^{-2}\sigma_{m,n}^{2}\bigr)
under the conditions.

For the first term (I), using a similar approach taken in
Section

C.11
, we may see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{I})^{2}]~=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\Gamma_{1}-\widehat{\Gamma}_{1}\bigr)^{4}\bigr]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\Biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{i}\,|\,X_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}\,|\,X_{i}]\bigr\}-\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\bigl\{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{i}\,|\,X_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}\,|\,X_{i}]\big\}\Biggr)^{4}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\big\{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[Y\,|\,X]-\mathbb{E}[Y\,|\,X]\big\}^{4}\bigr].

For the second term (II), we follow the notation given in
Section

C.11
and consider an inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{II})^{2}]\leq 2\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{II})_{1}^{2}]+2\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{II})_{2}^{2}].

Focusing on
(\mathrm{II})_{1}
, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality yields

\displaystyle\{\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{II})_{1}^{2}]\}^{2}~\leq~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{I})^{2}]\times\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}\,|\,X_{i}]\big\}+\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{j=1}^{n+m}\big\{\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}\,|\,X_{j}]-\mathbb{E}[Y]\big\}\biggr)^{4}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\big\{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[Y\,|\,X]-\mathbb{E}[Y\,|\,X]\big\}^{4}\bigr]\times\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}[Y^{4}]

and similarly, the term
(\mathrm{II})_{2}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{II})_{2}^{2}]\,\leq\,\frac{C}{n}\mu^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\big\{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[Y\,|\,X]-\mathbb{E}[Y\,|\,X]\big\}^{2}\bigr].

Therefore the second moment of the term
(\mathrm{II})
is bounded above by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{II})^{2}]\,\leq\,\frac{C_{1}}{n^{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[Y^{4}]\mathbb{E}\bigl[\big\{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[Y\,|\,X]-\mathbb{E}[Y\,|\,X]\big\}^{4}\bigr]}+\frac{C_{2}}{n}\mu^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\big\{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[Y\,|\,X]-\mathbb{E}[Y\,|\,X]\big\}^{2}\bigr].

Moreover, following the observations made in
Section

C.11
, the terms
(\mathrm{III})
and
(\mathrm{IV})
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{III})^{2}]\,\leq\,C_{3}\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{I})^{2}]\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\bigl\{\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{IV})^{2}]\bigr\}^{2}\,\leq\,C_{4}\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{III})^{2}]\times\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\Pi_{i,1}^{2}\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]\,\leq\,C_{5}\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{I})^{2}]\times\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}[Y^{4}].

Consequently, under the condition that
\mathbb{E}\bigl[\big\{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[Y\,|\,X]-\mathbb{E}[Y\,|\,X]\big\}^{4}\bigr]=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(U_{\mathrm{adapt}}-U_{\mathrm{adapt}}^{\star}\bigr)^{2}\bigr]=o_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(n^{-2}+n^{-2}\{\mathbb{E}[Y^{4}]\}^{1/2}+n^{-1}\mu^{2}\bigr).

Moreover, for any
n\geq 1,m\geq 0
, it holds that

\displaystyle 4n^{-1}\mu^{2}\sigma_{m,n}^{2}+2n^{-2}\sigma_{m,n}^{2}\geq 4n^{-1}\mu^{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(Y\,|\,X)]+2n^{-2}\bigl\{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(Y\,|\,X)]\bigr\}^{2},

which, together with the conditions
\mathbb{E}[Y^{4}]\leq C_{1}
and
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(Y\,|\,X)]\geq C_{2}
, implies

\displaystyle\frac{n^{-2}+n^{-2}\{\mathbb{E}[Y^{4}]\}^{1/2}+n^{-1}\mu^{2}}{4n^{-1}\mu^{2}\sigma_{m,n}^{2}+2n^{-2}\sigma_{m,n}^{2}}\leq C.

Hence, the limiting result (
28
) holds, which completes the proof of
Corollary

2
.

C.13
Proof of
Theorem

7

Recall that a random vector
(X,Y)
from
P_{XY}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{mean}}
has the relationship
Y=X+\varepsilon
where
X\sim N(\delta,\sigma_{X}^{2})
and
\varepsilon\sim N(c,\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})
are independent. Our goal is to find a local minimax lower bound for the MSE of estimating the squared expectation of
Y
denoted as
\mu^{2}=(c+\delta)^{2}
. Unlike the proofs for
Proposition

7
and
Theorem

5
, the current proof involves analyzing both a first-order lower bound and a second-order lower bound, converging to zero at
n
- and
n^{2}
-rates, respectively. The main idea behind obtaining the second-order lower bound is similar to that of the Bhattacharyya bound
(
Bhattacharyya, 1946
)
, which is a high-order extension of the Cramér–Rao lower bound.

Prior Construction.

In order to apply the van Trees inequality, we need to consider a prior distribution
g
of the parameters
c
and
\delta
. Denoting the first (resp. second) derivative of
g
as
g^{\prime}
(resp.
g^{\prime\prime}
), we assume that this prior distribution needs to satisfy the following conditions:

1.

g
is a proper density supported on the interval
[t_{0},t_{1}]
for
t_{0}<t_{1}
.

2.

g^{\prime}(t_{0})=g^{\prime}(t_{1})=0
and
g^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})=g^{\prime\prime}(t_{1})=0
.

3.
